> DEVELOPER

the alternative is we can rename inititaive-shape to something else? what does inititaive-shape do? just define goals? KRs?

> AGENT

Let me answer the factual question first, because it changes which rename even makes sense.

## What `initiative-shape` actually does

Not just goals/KRs — it's the full **bet-definition** skill. It takes a vague idea and produces a six-field initiative (a Linear project), behind gates:

1. **Goal** — the Objective ("for [who], we want to [outcome]")
2. **Key results** — 3 (cap 5), each with baseline / target / measured-over / how-we'll-know, a `(commit|stretch)` tag, and a `bet|brake|foundation` role
3. **Affected repos**
4. **Appetite** — ~N issues
5. **Kill condition**
6. **Project type** — the 1–6 taxonomy that selects the KR rubric

Plus the machinery: a problem-vs-solution gate, type-specific KR playbooks, an 11-rule verification rubric, and the Linear `save_project` write at the end.

So it owns **"is this worth committing to, and how will we know it worked"** — the OKR + appetite + kill condition. That's the unit that occupies a cycle slot.

## Why renaming it doesn't fix the collision

Here's the thing your question surfaces: **the new skill was never shaping "the project" in the first place.** There are three distinct concepts:

| Concept | Skill | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| The idea | `idea-triage` | pre-commitment screening |
| **The bet** | `initiative-shape` | goal + KRs + appetite + kill condition (= the Linear project) |
| **The plan** | *(new)* | how the committed bet breaks into deliverables → stories → tasks |

The new skill is **the plan** — it produces the delivery hierarchy *for* an initiative. The plan **belongs to** the initiative; it *isn't* the initiative. So "project-shape" was the wrong word from the start, regardless of what we call the bet layer. Renaming `initiative-shape` frees up the word "project," but the new skill still shouldn't claim it — because it's shaping delivery, not the bet.

## And renaming `initiative-shape` is expensive

"Initiative" isn't just this skill's name — it's the **load-bearing term of the entire governance model**. `linear-workflow.md` is built on it: the initiative model, initiative lifecycle, "3 initiatives + 1 ops slot," initiative size table. Renaming the skill means renaming the concept across `linear-workflow.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and every cross-reference — a wide blast radius for no real gain, since the collision is solved more cheaply by naming the *new* skill correctly.

## Recommendation

**Keep `initiative-shape` as-is. Name the new skill for the plan it produces.** Candidates:

- `delivery-shape` (lead) — shapes how the initiative gets delivered
- `plan-shape`
- `breakdown-shape`

One honest observation: every good name for the new skill crowds `planning-and-task-breakdown`'s territory (`plan`, `breakdown`, `delivery-of-tasks`). That crowding is itself **evidence for option (B)** — that this is an *upward expansion of `planning-and-task-breakdown`*, not a separate skill. Which is exactly what the walking-skeleton spike should settle.

So: nothing about `initiative-shape` needs to change. Want me to lock the name as `delivery-shape` and save the one-pager to `docs/ideas/delivery-shape.md` — or does the (B) signal make you want to hold the name until after the spike?

> DEVELOPER

thats ok i might take on the work to rename it - what other names could i choose to describe it better? in more product terms?

> AGENT

Good instinct to make it precise. First, a structural point that decides how far the rename reaches:

**The skill is named after the noun it produces.** `initiative-shape` outputs an "initiative." So this isn't really "rename a skill" — it's "rename the *unit that occupies a cycle slot*," and the skill name just follows. Whatever you pick, `linear-workflow.md`'s whole vocabulary changes with it (cycle = "3 initiatives + 1 ops slot," the size table, the lifecycle states). If you rename only the skill but keep calling the concept an "initiative" in governance, you've just created a new mismatch. So decide the *noun* first; the skill name is `<noun>-shape`.

Now, what is that noun, in product terms? The thing it produces is: **a desired outcome + measurable key results + an appetite + a kill condition + the decision to commit.** Candidates, ranked, each tied to a source the pack already leans on:

1. **`outcome` → `outcome-shape`** *(my pick)*. PRODUCT_RULES principle #1 is literally "outcomes, not outputs." The skill's entire job is to force outcome-framing before commitment. Reads cleanly as product vocabulary, and pairs beautifully with the delivery skill: **shape the outcome → shape the delivery.** Outcome-vs-delivery is a crisp, PM-literate split.

2. **`bet` → `bet-shape`**. Shape Up's "betting table"; PRODUCT_RULES principle #3 ("every roadmap item is a bet"). The skill already uses `bet|brake|foundation` KR roles and a kill condition — naming it `bet` makes the "you might lose this, and that's fine" honesty explicit. Slightly informal, but deeply on-brand for this pack.

3. **`objective` → `objective-shape`**. Pure OKR vocabulary — the skill calls the goal "the Objective" and is explicitly OKR-shaped (Wodtke). Accurate, but a touch dry/corporate, and it names half the artefact (the O, not the KRs or appetite).

4. **`goal` → `goal-shape`**. Simple and product-y, but undersells it — this is goal *plus* KRs, appetite, and kill condition, not just a goal. You'd lose the "measurable + bounded + killable" weight.

Weaker, noted so you can rule them out:
- **`commitment-shape`** — Cagan's "high-integrity commitment" is exactly right conceptually, but sounds heavy and slightly waterfall.
- **`pitch-shape`** — Shape Up's "pitch," but a pitch bundles *solution* shaping, which this skill deliberately refuses (it's problem/outcome-framed). Wrong connotation.
- **`okr-shape`** — names the *format*, not the purpose; you'd be naming it after the methodology rather than the thing.

My recommendation: **`outcome-shape`** for the bet layer, `delivery-shape` for the plan layer. It gives you the cleanest product narrative across the lifecycle:

> `idea-triage` → **`outcome-shape`** (what outcome are we betting on?) → **`delivery-shape`** (how do we deliver it?) → `planning-and-task-breakdown` (the slices)

If you want the probabilistic honesty front-and-centre over the outcomes-not-outputs framing, `bet-shape` is the strong second — and it's the only one that natively echoes the kill condition.

One caution before you commit: this is a wide find-and-replace across `linear-workflow.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `using-this-pack`, and every skill that cross-references `initiative-shape` (at minimum `idea-triage`, `planning-and-task-breakdown`, `roadmap-shape`, `backlog-manage`). Worth doing as its own tracked slice so the rename lands atomically and nothing references a dead name.

Want me to capture this naming decision in the one-pager's open questions, or are you leaning toward one so I can write it in as the assumed pair?

> DEVELOPER

ok lets keep inititiave for now, i'll make a separate plan to rename it to "outcome"

the workflow i'm thinking will be as you described:

idea-shape --> outcome-shape --> delivery-shape --> task-shape (planning-and-task-breakdown)

> AGENT

That's a clean, coherent vocabulary — and it reads as a proper product lifecycle: **shape the idea → shape the outcome → shape the delivery → shape the tasks.** Each `-shape` does one decision at one confidence stage.

One practical wrinkle to lock in before I save, because it bites agents directly (memory lives in artefacts, P7): three of those four are *renames of existing skills* and one (`delivery-shape`) is *new*. Until the renames land, the live chain is actually:

> `idea-triage` → `initiative-shape` → **`delivery-shape`** *(new)* → `planning-and-task-breakdown`

So `delivery-shape` must cross-reference the **current** names, or it'll point at skills that don't exist yet. The `-shape` vocabulary is the target; the references track reality until each rename ships.

That also suggests a clean split into **two separate initiatives** (by outcome, per `initiative-shape`'s own splitting rule):
- **This one** — ship the missing plan layer (`delivery-shape` + spike + discoverability).
- **A separate one** — unify the lifecycle vocabulary into the `-shape` family (`idea-triage`→`idea-shape`, `initiative`→`outcome`, `planning-and-task-breakdown`→`task-shape`). That's your deferred rename plan; it's independent and needn't block this.

NameDescriptionExamplesChained user storiesClassic user story format that explicitly traces value through the platform's intermediate user to the end outcome• "As a payments engineer integrating refunds, I want a single endpoint for partial refunds across split tenders, so support can issue refunds in one click, so customers don't churn after bad returns"• "As a checkout engineer, I want idempotent retry semantics, so I can safely re-submit on timeout, so customers aren't double-charged"Capability statementsDeclarative, system-as-subject requirements; no fictional user• "The platform must support idempotent transfer creation with client-provided keys and a 24-hour deduplication window"• "The platform must produce daily reconciliation between internal ledger and partner bank statements to sub-cent precision"SLOs / operational targetsNumeric, measurable targets for reliability, latency, accuracy, throughput• "Transfer API: 99.95% availability, p99 < 800ms, measured monthly; burn-rate alerts at 2x and 10x"• "Webhook delivery: 99.9% within 60s, at-least-once, exponential backoff over 24h before DLQ"ADRsArchitecture Decision Records capturing context, decision, alternatives considered, and consequences• "ADR-042: Double-entry ledger as source of truth for balances (not derived from transaction log)"• "ADR-051: Idempotency keys scoped per-merchant, not globally"Compliance obligationsRegulatory/policy-driven work framed as obligation + source + acceptance criteria + owner• "Travel Rule data on all transfers ≥ $3,000 USD (FinCEN 31 CFR 1010.410(f)); enforced at API, audit log retained 5 years"• "EFT error notification within 10 business days (Reg E §1005.11); dashboard tracks per-case latency, alerts at day 7"Enabler / foundation workSetup tasks required before feature work is possible: toolchain, CI, dev env, observability, runbooks• "Install Python 3.12 via mise, pin in .tool-versions, document setup in CONTRIBUTING.md"• "Stand up local dev via docker-compose: postgres, redis, mock partner bank endpoint"SpikesTime-boxed research where the output is a decision or document, not shipped code• "SPIKE: Evaluate Persona vs. in-house KYC. Time-box 3 days. Output: ADR + cost model"• "SPIKE: Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY vs. Kafka for webhook fanout. Time-box 1 week. Output: load test + decision"MigrationsMulti-phase moves between systems: dual-write, shadow-read, cutover, decommission, with rollback at each phase• "Migrate transfers from MySQL to ledger-v2: dual-write 4 wks, shadow-read 2 wks, flag cutover, decommission after 30d clean operation"• "Move secrets from .env files to Vault: inventory, dual-source 2 wks, cutover service-by-service, remove .env access"KTLO / maintenanceRecurring operational hygiene: dependency upgrades, cert/key rotation, patches, deprecated SDK bumps• "Q3 KTLO: Python 3.11 → 3.12 across services, rotate AWS keys, renew TLS certs expiring Oct 15"• "Monthly: bump deprecated SDK versions, patch base Docker images, prune dormant feature flags"Deprecations / sunsetsRemoving old systems, APIs, or features with notice period, migration support, gating, and final removal• "Deprecate v1 transfers API: 6-month notice, migrate top 10 integrators by volume, gate new integrations to v2, remove after 90d zero traffic"• "Sunset legacy webhook format: dual-emit 60d, integrator migration tracker, remove after all top-50 integrators upgraded"Incident remediationAction items from postmortems, scoped to a specific incident and its root cause• "INC-1247 follow-up: add idempotency check to refund flow (root cause: double-refund from retry storm)"• "INC-1283 follow-up: add circuit breaker on partner bank API (root cause: cascading timeouts during partner outage)"

> AGENT

Reviewed against the idea doc, the principles, and what the spike is meant to prove. Short version: **it fits, and it fixes a real weakness in the original idea — but not as "11 formats the skill authors." Reframe it as one idea: the node format is *polymorphic*, chosen by work type, and each type carries its own completion criterion.**

That reframing is the whole value. The original idea doc assumed a single shape — deliverable → capability → user-story-with-AC → task. Your list shows that's too rigid: a reconciliation requirement has no sensible end-user, a compliance item's "framing" is a regulatory citation, a migration's is a rollback-per-phase. Force a user story onto those and you get exactly the Mad-Libs theatre the pack warns against. So "framing baked in" generalises cleanly — **every node type has a framing, it's just type-specific**:

| Node type | Home in the pack | Its "framing" (completion criterion) | Grounding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chained user story | new (delivery-shape) | acceptance criteria | Cohn — but see flag |
| Capability statement | new | the declarative spec *is* the criterion | SAFe capability/NFR |
| SLO / op target | **Rule A4/A5** — delegate | the numeric target + fitness function | Google SRE |
| ADR | **documentation-and-adrs** — delegate | Consequences section | Nygard |
| Compliance obligation | new | source + AC + owner | SAFe enabler:compliance (roadmap A5) |
| Enabler / foundation | **our "foundational prompt"** — core | "skeleton runs" check | SAFe enabler:infrastructure |
| Spike | **Rule C5** — delegate | decision/doc + stop condition | SAFe enabler:exploration |
| Migration | new | rollback + per-phase verification gate | Fowler expand-contract |
| KTLO / maintenance | **ops slot** (linear-workflow) | *none — carve-out, no framing* | roadmap A5 |
| Deprecation / sunset | new | zero-traffic / migration-complete gate | API lifecycle |
| Incident remediation | **Rule D1** — delegate | root cause addressed + guard test | Google SRE postmortem |

The operational consequence: **delivery-shape's job is to *select the right node type and delegate*, not re-author each format.** Six of these eleven already have owners in the pack (SLOs, ADRs, spikes, incident actions, KTLO, foundation). If the skill re-implements them it drifts from those owners — the exact gate-drift the idea doc's delegate-don't-reimplement rule forbids. The skill's unique contribution is **selection + composition + refusing to silently drop the unglamorous types** (foundation, compliance, KTLO — the ones you said you forget).

Three things I'd push back on before this is settled:

1. **"Chained user stories" is the least-grounded item.** The double-"so… so…" form is bespoke. The grounded options are Cohn's classic ("…so that [benefit]") or Intercom's **job story** ("When [situation], I want [motivation], so I can [outcome]"). The job story is actually a better fit for your platform/intermediate-user case because it drops the fictional persona. I'd ground it on one of those rather than ship a custom format.

2. **The calendar-time examples split two ways (agentic P8).** "SPIKE: time-box 3 days" is *effort in calendar* — forbidden; it should be slices + a stop condition (that's literally how I wrote ABA-281/282). But "migration soak: 30d clean operation," "deprecation: 6-month notice," "Reg E: 10 business days" are *external/observation windows* — P8 explicitly permits calendar at the human-interface seam. The contract should make delivery-shape distinguish the two, not blanket-ban dates.

3. **Stack-agnostic line (CLAUDE.md hard rule).** Your examples name tools — mise, docker-compose, Vault, Persona, Kafka. The *emitted artefacts* naming tools is fine (that's the user's real plan). The *skill's templates* naming them is not. Clean line to hold.

One type the list is **missing**, and it's grounded in your own product rules: **experiment / validation** (Gilad GIST, Torres OST, Rule B6). A spike outputs a *decision*; an experiment outputs *evidence from live users*. For product (not platform) initiatives the leaf is often "ship an A/B test to validate hypothesis H" — distinct from all eleven above.

**How it lands in the work:** this enriches the spike's contract artefact (ABA-281's `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md`) — it doesn't change the gate. The contract should prove the file-set can express *polymorphic* node types, each with a `type` tag, a completion-criterion form, and a delegation target — not just user stories. To keep the spike thin and avoid inventing: pick a real example that naturally exercises a *handful* of these types, prove polymorphism works, and list the fuller vocabulary as a contract appendix filled in as real examples surface — don't fabricate all twelve from an example that only shows three. It also nudges the A-vs-B call toward **(A) new skill** (twelve delegating node types is a lot of surface for an *expansion* of planning-and-task-breakdown) — but that's the gate's decision, on evidence, not now.

Want me to fold this into ABA-281 — add an acceptance criterion that the contract defines a polymorphic node-type vocabulary (type tag + completion-criterion form + delegation target), grounded in the example, with the rest as a to-fill appendix?

> AGENT

In plain terms:

**Worth folding in (genuinely new):**
- **The big one — "different work needs different formats."** Your original plan only knew how to write user stories. Your list proves that's too narrow: a compliance item, a migration, a cleanup job each need their own shape. That's the real upgrade. Fold it.
- **One format you missed: experiments.** "Ship an A/B test to see if this works" is different from research and from building. Add it.

**Already covered — don't re-add, just point at what exists:**
- SLOs, ADRs, spikes, incident fixes, routine maintenance (KTLO), foundational setup work. The pack already has homes for all of these. The skill should *hand off* to them, not rewrite them. Copying them in is how things drift out of sync.

**Fix before folding (don't take as-is):**
- **"Chained user stories"** — your custom "so… so…" format is homemade. Use a standard one instead (e.g. the "job story" format, which also drops the fake persona you don't really have). Small change, better grounding.
- **The day/week labels in your examples** — "time-box 3 days" is the kind of made-up estimate we don't do. But "6-month deprecation notice" or "10-business-day legal deadline" are real external dates and stay. The skill needs to tell those two apart.
- **Tool names (mise, Vault, Kafka, etc.)** — fine in the actual plans you produce; not allowed in the skill's templates (it has to stay tool-agnostic).

**Net:** one real addition (polymorphic formats), one missing format (experiments), one rename (job story), two guardrails (real-dates-vs-fake-estimates, no tool names in the skill). Everything else you listed is already in the box.

Want me to fold that into the spike issue (ABA-281)?

> AGENT

Both updated.

**ABA-281** now carries the genuinely-new bits:
- **Nodes are polymorphic** — pick the format that fits the work, not always a user story.
- **Each node type lists its own completion criterion + delegation target** — so SLOs/ADRs/spikes/incident/KTLO hand off to their existing owners instead of being re-authored.
- **Experiment** added as a node type (hypothesis + success metric).
- Grounding guardrails baked in: job-story not the custom chain · real external dates allowed, fake effort-in-days not · tool-agnostic templates · **don't fabricate node types the example doesn't show** (only ground what the example exercises; rest goes in a to-fill appendix).

**ABA-282** got the balancing **A-signal** added to the gate: the polymorphic vocabulary is a lot of surface to bolt onto a task-breakdown skill — which counterweights the existing B-signal, so the new-skill-vs-expand call stays honest.

That's the fold done. The spike's shape is unchanged — still "produce a real file-set, read the contract off it, prove it walks and converts" — it just now has to prove the contract handles *polymorphic* nodes, not only stories.

One thing I noticed in the response but didn't touch: both issues picked up `model:opus` / `review:high` labels and self-assigned to you — looks like a Linear automation, not me. Leave it as-is?

Quick pass, applying Strunk's rules (13 omit needless words, 10 active voice, 12 concrete). Verdict: **both are tight** — the bodies already read like edited prose. Only a few micro-trims, none essential:

**ABA-281**
- *Rule 13* — "feeds goal+KRs **in** as text" → "feeds goal+KRs as text" (drop "in").
- *Rule 13* — "the types the example **actually** exercises" → "the types the example exercises" ("actually" adds nothing).
- *Rule 10 (judgment call)* — "If a committed initiative's full hierarchy **can't be expressed as** cross-referenced markdown…" is passive. Active: "If we can't express a committed initiative's full hierarchy as cross-referenced markdown…" Slightly punchier, but the passive keeps "hierarchy" as the subject, which reads fine here. Marginal.

**ABA-282**
- Clean. The only soft spot is "is **a lot of surface** to bolt onto a task-breakdown skill" — colloquial, but concrete and clear, so I'd leave it. No changes.

So: two no-brainer word deletions in 281, one optional voice swap. Want me to apply the two trims (and the voice swap or not)?

  "description": "Part 1 of the walking-skeleton spike. Parked-safe: feeds goal+KRs as text, so it does **not** wait on the `initiative-shape` Linear-decouple.\n\n**Why this is first.** It is the riskiest assumption and the kill gate. If a committed initiative's full hierarchy can't be expressed as cross-referenced markdown that a human reads top-down and a script can walk, the standalone-markdown premise is wrong — see the project's kill condition. Test it before building anything (eng-universal P4, Rule B2).\n\n**Do:** by hand, take one real already-shaped initiative (this project, or another) and produce the full delivery hierarchy as a markdown file-set — deliverable → capability → node → task. **Nodes are polymorphic**: pick the format that fits the work, not always a user story — story-with-AC, capability statement, SLO, compliance obligation, migration, deprecation, experiment, and so on. Walking-skeleton task first; foundational/toolchain setup folded in, never silent. Then read the schema off the example into `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md`.\n\n**Acceptance criteria:**\n- `examples/delivery-plans/<initiative>/` exists; a human can read it top-down through all layers.\n- Every node carries its **type-appropriate** completion criterion — story → AC, capability → the spec itself, SLO → numeric target, spike → decision + stop condition, migration → rollback per phase, experiment → hypothesis + success metric, KTLO → none (carve-out, roadmap A5). At least one foundational/skeleton task is present.\n- `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` names the three schema elements read off the example: directory layout · cross-reference convention (front-matter links / relative paths / manifest) · per-node tags (node `type`, deliverable↔KR, completion-criterion, task↔skeleton flag).\n- The contract defines a **node-type vocabulary**: each type lists its completion-criterion form and its delegation target — SLO → Rule A4/A5, ADR → documentation-and-adrs, spike → Rule C5, incident → Rule D1, KTLO → ops slot. delivery-shape *selects and delegates*; it does not re-author these. Ground the vocabulary in the types the example exercises; list the rest as a to-fill appendix — **do not fabricate types the example doesn't show**.\n\n**Constraints on the contract:**\n- Use a grounded story format (Cohn / job story), not a custom \"so… so…\" chain.\n- Distinguish external windows (notice periods, legal deadlines, soak time — allowed) from effort-in-days (forbidden, agentic P8).\n- Templates stay tool-agnostic; emitted artefacts may name tools, the contract may not.\n\n**Tests:** the \"hierarchy expresses as walkable markdown\" half of the kill condition.\n\n**KR1** artefacts 1 (contract) + 2 (worked file-set). Artefact 3 (walk-script) and the mechanical-conversion proof are the gate issue (ABA-282)."
}
